       1 | # Singly Linked List with common operations: push, delete, reverse,
       2 | # find middle (Floyd's slow/fast pointer), and cycle detection.
       3 | class Node
       4 |   attr_accessor :value, :next_node
       5 |   def initialize(value)
       6 |     @value = value
       7 |     @next_node = nil
       8 |   end
       9 | end
      10 | 
      11 | class LinkedList
      12 |   include Enumerable
      13 |   attr_reader :head
      14 | 
      15 |   def initialize
      16 |     @head = nil
      17 |     @tail = nil
      18 |     @size = 0
      19 |   end
      20 | 
      21 |   def push(value)
      22 |     node = Node.new(value)
      23 |     if @head.nil?
      24 |       @head = @tail = node
      25 |     else
      26 |       @tail.next_node = node
      27 |       @tail = node
      28 |     end
      29 |     @size += 1
      30 |     self
      31 |   end
      32 |   alias_method :<<, :push
      33 | 
      34 |   def each
      35 |     return enum_for(:each) unless block_given?
      36 |     node = @head
      37 |     while node
      38 |       yield node.value
      39 |       node = node.next_node
      40 |     end
      41 |   end
      42 | 
      43 |   def delete(value)
      44 |     return false if @head.nil?
      45 |     if @head.value == value
      46 |       @head = @head.next_node
      47 |       @size -= 1
      48 |       return true
      49 |     end
      50 |     prev = @head
      51 |     curr = @head.next_node
      52 |     while curr
      53 |       if curr.value == value
      54 |         prev.next_node = curr.next_node
      55 |         @tail = prev if curr == @tail
      56 |         @size -= 1
      57 |         return true
      58 |       end
      59 |       prev = curr
      60 |       curr = curr.next_node
      61 |     end
      62 |     false
      63 |   end
      64 | 
      65 |   def reverse!
      66 |     prev = nil
      67 |     curr = @head
      68 |     @tail = @head
      69 |     while curr
      70 |       nxt = curr.next_node
      71 |       curr.next_node = prev
      72 |       prev = curr
      73 |       curr = nxt
      74 |     end
      75 |     @head = prev
      76 |     self
      77 |   end
      78 | 
      79 |   def middle
      80 |     slow = fast = @head
      81 |     while fast && fast.next_node
      82 |       slow = slow.next_node
      83 |       fast = fast.next_node.next_node
>>>   84 |     end
      85 |     slow&.value
      86 |   end
      87 | 
      88 |   def has_cycle?
      89 |     slow = fast = @head
      90 |     while fast && fast.next_node
      91 |       slow = slow.next_node
      92 |       fast = fast.next_node.next_node
      93 |       return true if slow == fast
      94 |     end
      95 |     false
      96 |   end
      97 | 
      98 |   def size
      99 |     @size
     100 |   end
     101 | end
     102 | 
     103 | if __FILE__ == $0
     104 |   list = LinkedList.new
     105 |   [1, 2, 3, 4, 5].each { |v| list << v }
     106 |   puts "List: #{list.to_a}"
     107 |   puts "Middle element: #{list.middle}"
     108 |   list.delete(3)
     109 |   puts "After deleting 3: #{list.to_a}"
     110 |   list.reverse!
     111 |   puts "Reversed: #{list.to_a}"
     112 |   puts "Has cycle? #{list.has_cycle?}"

       1 | # Array-backed Stack used to check whether brackets in an expression
       2 | # are balanced (classic compiler/parser building block).
       3 | class Stack
       4 |   def initialize
       5 |     @data = []
       6 |   end
       7 | 
       8 |   def push(x)
       9 |     @data.push(x)
      10 |   end
      11 | 
      12 |   def pop
      13 |     @data.pop
      14 |   end
      15 | 
      16 |   def peek
      17 |     @data.last
      18 |   end
      19 | 
      20 |   def empty?
      21 |     @data.empty?
      22 |   end
      23 | 
      24 |   def size
      25 |     @data.size
      26 |   end
      27 | end
      28 | 
      29 | def balanced?(expression)
      30 |   stack = Stack.new
      31 |   pairs = { ')' => '(', ']' => '[', '}' => '{' }
      32 |   expression.each_char do |ch|
      33 |     if '([{'.include?(ch)
      34 |       stack.push(ch)
      35 |     elsif ')]}'.include?(ch)
      36 |       return false if stack.empty? || stack.pop != pairs[ch]
      37 |     end
      38 |   end
      39 |   stack.empty?
      40 | end

      43 |   tests = ['(a+b)*[c-d]', '([)]', '{[()()]}', '((()']
      44 |   tests.each { |t| puts "#{t.ljust(15)} => balanced? #{balanced?(t)}" }
      45 | end

       1 | # Fixed-capacity circular (ring) buffer queue with O(1) enqueue/dequeue.
       2 | class CircularQueue
       3 |   def initialize(capacity)
       4 |     @capacity = capacity
       5 |     @data = Array.new(capacity)
       6 |     @head = 0
       7 |     @tail = 0
       8 |     @count = 0
       9 |   end
      10 | 
      11 |   def full?
      12 |     @count == @capacity
      13 |   end
      14 | 
      15 |   def empty?
      16 |     @count.zero?
      17 |   end
      18 | 
      19 |   def enqueue(value)
      20 |     raise "Queue full" if full?
      21 |     @data[@tail] = value
      22 |     @tail = (@tail + 1) % @capacity
      23 |     @count += 1
      24 |     self
      25 |   end
      26 | 
      27 |   def dequeue
      28 |     raise "Queue empty" if empty?
      29 |     value = @data[@head]
      30 |     @head = (@head + 1) % @capacity
      31 |     @count -= 1
      32 |     value
      33 |   end
      34 | 
      35 |   def to_a
      36 |     (0...@count).map { |i| @data[(@head + i) % @capacity] }
      37 |   end
      38 | end
      39 | 
      40 | if __FILE__ == $0
      41 |   q = CircularQueue.new(5)
>>>   42 |   [1, 2, 3, 4, 5].each { |v| q.enqueue(v) }
      43 |   puts "Queue: #{q.to_a}"
      44 |   puts "Dequeued: #{q.dequeue}, #{q.dequeue}"
      45 |   q.enqueue(6).enqueue(7)
      46 |   puts "Queue after wraparound: #{q.to_a}"

       1 | # Unbalanced Binary Search Tree with insert, search, delete (including
       2 | # the two-children successor case), inorder traversal, and height.
       3 | class BSTNode
       4 |   attr_accessor :value, :left, :right
       5 |   def initialize(value)
       6 |     @value = value
       7 |   end
       8 | end
       9 | 
      10 | class BST
      11 |   def initialize
      12 |     @root = nil
      13 |   end
      14 | 
      15 |   def insert(value)
      16 |     @root = insert_node(@root, value)
      17 |     self
      18 |   end
      19 | 
      20 |   def search(value)
      21 |     node = @root
      22 |     while node
      23 |       return true if node.value == value
      24 |       node = value < node.value ? node.left : node.right
      25 |     end
      26 |     false
      27 |   end
      28 | 
      29 |   def delete(value)
      30 |     @root = delete_node(@root, value)
      31 |   end

>>>   35 |     traverse(@root) { |v| result << v }
      36 |     result
      37 |   end
      38 | 
      39 |   def height(node = @root)
      40 |     return -1 if node.nil?
      41 |     1 + [height(node.left), height(node.right)].max
      42 |   end
      43 | 
      44 |   private
      45 | 
      46 |   def insert_node(node, value)
      47 |     return BSTNode.new(value) if node.nil?
      48 |     if value < node.value
      49 |       node.left = insert_node(node.left, value)
      50 |     elsif value > node.value
      51 |       node.right = insert_node(node.right, value)
      52 |     end
      53 |     node
      54 |   end
      55 | 
      56 |   def delete_node(node, value)
      57 |     return nil if node.nil?
      58 |     if value < node.value
      59 |       node.left = delete_node(node.left, value)
      60 |     elsif value > node.value
      61 |       node.right = delete_node(node.right, value)
      62 |     else
      63 |       return node.right if node.left.nil?
      64 |       return node.left if node.right.nil?
      65 |       successor = node.right
      66 |       successor = successor.left while successor.left
      67 |       node.value = successor.value
      68 |       node.right = delete_node(node.right, successor.value)
      69 |     end
      70 |     node
      71 |   end
      72 | 
      73 |   def traverse(node, &block)
      74 |     return unless node
      75 |     traverse(node.left, &block)
      76 |     block.call(node.value)
      77 |     traverse(node.right, &block)
      78 |   end
      79 | end

       1 | # Self-balancing AVL Tree. Maintains O(log n) height via rotations
       2 | # after every insert, unlike a plain BST which can degrade to O(n).
       3 | class AVLNode
       4 |   attr_accessor :value, :left, :right, :height
       5 |   def initialize(value)
       6 |     @value = value
       7 |     @height = 1
       8 |   end
       9 | end
      10 | 
      11 | class AVLTree
      12 |   def insert(value)
      13 |     @root = insert_node(@root, value)
      14 |     self
      15 |   end
      16 | 
      17 |   def inorder
      18 |     result = []
>>>   19 |     traverse(@root) { |v| result << v }
      20 |     result
      21 |   end
      22 | 
      23 |   def balance_factor(node)
      24 |     node ? height(node.left) - height(node.right) : 0
      25 |   end
      26 | 
      27 |   private
      28 | 
      29 |   def height(node)
      30 |     node ? node.height : 0
      31 |   end
      32 | 
      33 |   def update_height(node)
      34 |     node.height = 1 + [height(node.left), height(node.right)].max
      35 |   end
      36 | 
      37 |   def rotate_right(y)
      38 |     x = y.left
      39 |     t2 = x.right
      40 |     x.right = y
      41 |     y.left = t2
      42 |     update_height(y)
      43 |     update_height(x)
      44 |     x
      45 |   end
      46 | 
      47 |   def rotate_left(x)
      48 |     y = x.right
      49 |     t2 = y.left
      50 |     y.left = x
      51 |     x.right = t2
      52 |     update_height(x)
      53 |     update_height(y)
      54 |     y
      55 |   end
      56 | 
      57 |   def insert_node(node, value)
      58 |     return AVLNode.new(value) if node.nil?
      59 |     if value < node.value
      60 |       node.left = insert_node(node.left, value)
      61 |     elsif value > node.value
      62 |       node.right = insert_node(node.right, value)
      63 |     else
      64 |       return node
      65 |     end
      66 | 
      67 |     update_height(node)
      68 |     bf = balance_factor(node)
      69 | 
      70 |     return rotate_right(node) if bf > 1 && value < node.left.value
      71 |     return rotate_left(node) if bf < -1 && value > node.right.value
      72 |     if bf > 1 && value > node.left.value
      73 |       node.left = rotate_left(node.left)
      74 |       return rotate_right(node)
      75 |     end
      76 |     if bf < -1 && value < node.right.value
      77 |       node.right = rotate_right(node.right)
      78 |       return rotate_left(node)
      79 |     end
      80 |     node
      81 |   end
      82 | 
      83 |   def traverse(node, &block)
      84 |     return unless node
      85 |     traverse(node.left, &block)
      86 |     block.call(node.value)
      87 |     traverse(node.right, &block)
      88 |   end
      89 | end

       1 | # Binary heap implemented on an array, parameterized by a comparator
       2 | # so it can act as either a min-heap or max-heap. Used to build heapsort.
       3 | class BinaryHeap
       4 |   def initialize(&comparator)
       5 |     @data = []
       6 |     @cmp = comparator || ->(a, b) { a <=> b }
       7 |   end
       8 | 
       9 |   def push(value)
      10 |     @data << value
      11 |     sift_up(@data.size - 1)
      12 |     self
      13 |   end
      14 | 
      15 |   def pop
      16 |     return nil if @data.empty?
      17 |     top = @data[0]
      18 |     last = @data.pop
      19 |     unless @data.empty?
      20 |       @data[0] = last
      21 |       sift_down(0)
      22 |     end
      23 |     top
      24 |   end
      25 | 
      26 |   def peek
      27 |     @data[0]
      28 |   end
      29 | 
      30 |   def empty?
      31 |     @data.empty?
      32 |   end
      33 | 
      34 |   def size
      35 |     @data.size
      36 |   end
      37 | 
      38 |   private
      39 | 
      40 |   def sift_up(i)
      41 |     while i > 0
      42 |       parent = (i - 1) / 2
      43 |       break if @cmp.call(@data[i], @data[parent]) >= 0
      44 |       @data[i], @data[parent] = @data[parent], @data[i]
      45 |       i = parent
      46 |     end
      47 |   end
      48 | 
      49 |   def sift_down(i)
      50 |     loop do
      51 |       left, right, smallest = 2 * i + 1, 2 * i + 2, i
      52 |       smallest = left if left < @data.size && @cmp.call(@data[left], @data[smallest]) < 0
      53 |       smallest = right if right < @data.size && @cmp.call(@data[right], @data[smallest]) < 0
      54 |       break if smallest == i
      55 |       @data[i], @data[smallest] = @data[smallest], @data[i]
      56 |       i = smallest
      57 |     end
      58 |   end
      59 | end
      60 | 
      61 | def heapsort(array)
      62 |   heap = BinaryHeap.new
      63 |   array.each { |v| heap.push(v) }
      64 |   result = []
      65 |   result << heap.pop until heap.empty?
      66 |   result
      67 | end
      68 | 
      69 | if __FILE__ == $0
>>>   70 |   min_heap = BinaryHeap.new
      71 |   [5, 3, 8, 1, 9, 2].each { |v| min_heap.push(v) }
      72 |   puts "Min popped in order: #{Array.new(6) { min_heap.pop }}"
      73 | 
      74 |   max_heap = BinaryHeap.new { |a, b| b <=> a }

       1 | # Disjoint Set (Union-Find) with path compression and union by rank,
       2 | # giving near-O(1) amortized find/union operations.
       3 | class UnionFind
       4 |   def initialize(n)
       5 |     @parent = Array.new(n) { |i| i }
       6 |     @rank = Array.new(n, 0)
       7 |     @count = n
       8 |   end
       9 | 
      10 |   def find(x)
      11 |     while @parent[x] != x
      12 |       @parent[x] = @parent[@parent[x]] # path compression (halving)
      13 |       x = @parent[x]
      14 |     end
      15 |     x
      16 |   end
      17 | 
      18 |   def union(x, y)
      19 |     root_x, root_y = find(x), find(y)
      20 |     return false if root_x == root_y
      21 | 
      22 |     root_x, root_y = root_y, root_x if @rank[root_x] < @rank[root_y]
      23 |     @parent[root_y] = root_x
      24 |     @rank[root_x] += 1 if @rank[root_x] == @rank[root_y]
      25 |     @count -= 1
      26 |     true
      27 |   end
      28 | 
      29 |   def connected?(x, y)
      30 |     find(x) == find(y)
      31 |   end
      32 | 
      33 |   def component_count
      34 |     @count
      35 |   end
      36 | end
      37 | 
      38 | if __FILE__ == $0
      39 |   uf = UnionFind.new(10)
      40 |   [[0, 1], [1, 2], [3, 4], [5, 6], [6, 7]].each { |a, b| uf.union(a, b) }
      41 |   puts "0 and 2 connected? #{uf.connected?(0, 2)}"
      42 |   puts "0 and 3 connected? #{uf.connected?(0, 3)}"
      43 |   puts "Number of components: #{uf.component_count}"
      44 |   uf.union(2, 3)
      45 |   puts "After union(2,3), 0 and 4 connected? #{uf.connected?(0, 4)}"
>>>   46 |   puts "Number of components: #{uf.component_count}"
      47 | end

       1 | # LRU (Least Recently Used) Cache combining a Hash for O(1) lookup with
       2 | # a doubly linked list for O(1) reordering/eviction on access.
       3 | class LRUCache
>>>    4 |   Node = Struct.new(:key, :value, :prev_node, :next_node)
       5 | 
       6 |   def initialize(capacity)
       7 |     @capacity = capacity
       8 |     @map = {}
       9 |     @head = Node.new(:head, nil) # dummy head
      10 |     @tail = Node.new(:tail, nil) # dummy tail
      11 |     @head.next_node = @tail
      12 |     @tail.prev_node = @head
      13 |   end
      14 | 
      15 |   def get(key)
      16 |     node = @map[key]
      17 |     return -1 unless node
      18 |     move_to_front(node)
      19 |     node.value
      20 |   end
      21 | 
      22 |   def put(key, value)
      23 |     if (node = @map[key])
      24 |       node.value = value
      25 |       move_to_front(node)
      26 |     else
      27 |       node = Node.new(key, value)
      28 |       @map[key] = node
      29 |       insert_front(node)
      30 |       if @map.size > @capacity
      31 |         lru = @tail.prev_node
      32 |         remove(lru)
      33 |         @map.delete(lru.key)
      34 |       end
      35 |     end
      36 |   end
      37 | 
      38 |   def to_a
      39 |     result = []
      40 |     node = @head.next_node
      41 |     while node != @tail
      42 |       result << [node.key, node.value]
      43 |       node = node.next_node
      44 |     end
      45 |     result
      46 |   end
      47 | 
      48 |   private
      49 | 
      50 |   def remove(node)
      51 |     node.prev_node.next_node = node.next_node
      52 |     node.next_node.prev_node = node.prev_node
      53 |   end
      54 | 
      55 |   def insert_front(node)
      56 |     node.next_node = @head.next_node
      57 |     node.prev_node = @head
      58 |     @head.next_node.prev_node = node
      59 |     @head.next_node = node
      60 |   end
      61 | 
      62 |   def move_to_front(node)
      63 |     remove(node)
      64 |     insert_front(node)
      65 |   end
      66 | end
      67 | 
      68 | if __FILE__ == $0
      69 |   cache = LRUCache.new(3)
      70 |   cache.put(1, 'a')
      71 |   cache.put(2, 'b')
      72 |   cache.put(3, 'c')
      73 |   puts "Cache: #{cache.to_a}"
      74 |   cache.get(1) # touches 1, making it most recently used
      75 |   cache.put(4, 'd') # evicts 2 (least recently used)
      76 |   puts "After accessing 1 and inserting 4: #{cache.to_a}"
      77 |   puts "Get 2 (evicted): #{cache.get(2)}"
      78 | end

>>>    4 |     @adj = Hash.new { |h, k| h[k] = [] }
       5 |   end
       6 |

       8 |     @adj[u] << v
       9 |     @adj[v] << u unless directed
      10 |     self
      11 |   end
      12 | 
      13 |   def bfs(start)
      14 |     visited = { start => true }
      15 |     queue = [start]
      16 |     order = []
      17 |     until queue.empty?
      18 |       node = queue.shift
      19 |       order << node
      20 |       @adj[node].each do |neighbor|
      21 |         unless visited[neighbor]
      22 |           visited[neighbor] = true
      23 |           queue << neighbor
      24 |         end
      25 |       end
      26 |     end
      27 |     order
      28 |   end
      29 |

      31 |     visited = {}
      32 |     order = []

      34 |     order
      35 |   end
      36 | 
      37 |   private
      38 | 
      39 |   def dfs_visit(node, visited, order)
      40 |     return if visited[node]
      41 |     visited[node] = true
      42 |     order << node
      43 |     @adj[node].each { |neighbor| dfs_visit(neighbor, visited, order) }
      44 |   end
      45 | end

       1 | # Dijkstra's single-source shortest path algorithm on a weighted graph.
       2 | # Uses a simple array-based priority queue -- fine for teaching-scale
       3 | # graphs; swap in a binary heap for production-scale performance.
       4 | require 'set'
       5 | 
       6 | class WeightedGraph
       7 |   def initialize
>>>    8 |     @adj = Hash.new { |h, k| h[k] = [] }
       9 |   end
      10 | 
      11 |   def add_edge(u, v, weight)
      12 |     @adj[u] << [v, weight]
      13 |     @adj[v] << [u, weight]
      14 |     self
      15 |   end
      16 | 
      17 |   def dijkstra(source)
      18 |     dist = Hash.new(Float::INFINITY)
      19 |     dist[source] = 0
      20 |     visited = Set.new
      21 |     pq = [[0, source]]
      22 | 
      23 |     until pq.empty?
      24 |       pq.sort_by! { |d, _| d }
      25 |       d, u = pq.shift
      26 |       next if visited.include?(u)
      27 |       visited << u
      28 | 
      29 |       @adj[u].each do |v, weight|
      30 |         next if visited.include?(v)
      31 |         new_dist = d + weight
      32 |         if new_dist < dist[v]
      33 |           dist[v] = new_dist
      34 |           pq << [new_dist, v]
      35 |         end
      36 |       end
      37 |     end
      38 |     dist
      39 |   end
      40 | end
      41 | 
      42 | if __FILE__ == $0
      43 |   g = WeightedGraph.new
      44 |   g.add_edge('A', 'B', 4)
      45 |   g.add_edge('A', 'C', 1)
      46 |   g.add_edge('C', 'B', 2)
      47 |   g.add_edge('B', 'D', 5)
      48 |   g.add_edge('C', 'D', 8)
      49 |   dist = g.dijkstra('A')

       1 | # Bellman-Ford: handles negative edge weights and detects
       2 | # negative-weight cycles, unlike Dijkstra.
       3 | class BellmanFord
       4 |   Edge = Struct.new(:from, :to, :weight)
       5 | 
       6 |   def initialize(vertices)
       7 |     @vertices = vertices
       8 |     @edges = []
       9 |   end
      10 | 
>>>   11 |   def add_edge(from, to, weight)
      12 |     @edges << Edge.new(from, to, weight)
      13 |     self
      14 |   end
      15 | 
      16 |   def shortest_paths(source)
      17 |     dist = Hash.new(Float::INFINITY)
      18 |     dist[source] = 0
      19 | 
      20 |     (@vertices.size - 1).times do
      21 |       @edges.each do |e|
      22 |         if dist[e.from] + e.weight < dist[e.to]
      23 |           dist[e.to] = dist[e.from] + e.weight
      24 |         end
      25 |       end
      26 |     end
      27 | 
      28 |     @edges.each do |e|
      29 |       if dist[e.from] + e.weight < dist[e.to]
      30 |         return { error: "Graph contains a negative-weight cycle" }
      31 |       end
      32 |     end
      33 | 
      34 |     dist
      35 |   end
      36 | end
      37 | 
      38 | if __FILE__ == $0

       1 | # Floyd-Warshall all-pairs shortest path via dynamic programming
       2 | # over an adjacency matrix. O(V^3), works with negative edges
       3 | # (but not negative cycles).
       4 | INF = Float::INFINITY
       5 | 
       6 | def floyd_warshall(matrix)
       7 |   n = matrix.size
       8 |   dist = matrix.map(&:dup)
       9 | 
      10 |   (0...n).each do |k|
      11 |     (0...n).each do |i|
      12 |       (0...n).each do |j|
      13 |         if dist[i][k] + dist[k][j] < dist[i][j]
      14 |           dist[i][j] = dist[i][k] + dist[k][j]
      15 |         end
      16 |       end
      17 |     end
      18 |   end
      19 |   dist
      20 | end

       2 | # edges that don't form a cycle (checked via Union-Find).
       3 | class UnionFindMST

       5 |     @parent = Array.new(n) { |i| i }

      11 |   end

       1 | # Prim's Minimum Spanning Tree: grow a tree from an arbitrary root,
       2 | # always adding the cheapest edge that connects a new vertex.
       3 | def prim(num_vertices, adj)
       4 |   # adj: hash of vertex => [[neighbor, weight], ...]
       5 |   visited = Array.new(num_vertices, false)
       6 |   min_edge = Array.new(num_vertices, Float::INFINITY)
       7 |   parent = Array.new(num_vertices, -1)
       8 |   min_edge[0] = 0
       9 |   total_weight = 0
      10 |   mst_edges = []
      11 | 
      12 |   num_vertices.times do
      13 |     u = -1
      14 |     (0...num_vertices).each do |v|
      15 |       next if visited[v]
      16 |       u = v if u == -1 || min_edge[v] < min_edge[u]
      17 |     end
      18 |     break if min_edge[u] == Float::INFINITY
      19 | 
      20 |     visited[u] = true
      21 |     total_weight += min_edge[u]
      22 |     mst_edges << [parent[u], u, min_edge[u]] if parent[u] != -1
      23 | 
      24 |     adj[u].each do |v, weight|
      25 |       if !visited[v] && weight < min_edge[v]
      26 |         min_edge[v] = weight
      27 |         parent[v] = u
      28 |       end
      29 |     end
      30 |   end
      31 |   [mst_edges, total_weight]
      32 | end
      33 | 
      34 | if __FILE__ == $0
>>>   35 |   adj = Hash.new { |h, k| h[k] = [] }

      39 |   mst, weight = prim(9, adj)

       1 | # Topological sort of a DAG via two classic methods: Kahn's algorithm
       2 | # (BFS on in-degrees) and a DFS-based post-order approach.
       3 | def topo_sort_kahn(num_vertices, edges)
>>>    4 |   adj = Hash.new { |h, k| h[k] = [] }
       5 |   in_degree = Array.new(num_vertices, 0)
       6 |   edges.each do |u, v|
       7 |     adj[u] << v
       8 |     in_degree[v] += 1
       9 |   end
      10 | 
      11 |   queue = (0...num_vertices).select { |v| in_degree[v].zero? }
      12 |   order = []
      13 |   until queue.empty?
      14 |     u = queue.shift
      15 |     order << u
      16 |     adj[u].each do |v|
      17 |       in_degree[v] -= 1
      18 |       queue << v if in_degree[v].zero?
      19 |     end
      20 |   end
      21 | 
      22 |   raise "Graph has a cycle" if order.size != num_vertices
      23 |   order
      24 | end
      25 | 
      26 | def topo_sort_dfs(num_vertices, edges)
      27 |   adj = Hash.new { |h, k| h[k] = [] }
      28 |   edges.each { |u, v| adj[u] << v }
      29 |   visited = Array.new(num_vertices, false)
      30 |   stack = []
      31 | 
      32 |   visit = lambda do |u|
      33 |     visited[u] = true
      34 |     adj[u].each { |v| visit.call(v) unless visited[v] }
      35 |     stack.push(u)
      36 |   end
      37 | 
      38 |   (0...num_vertices).each { |v| visit.call(v) unless visited[v] }
      39 |   stack.reverse
      40 | end
      41 | 
      42 | if __FILE__ == $0
      43 |   edges = [[5, 2], [5, 0], [4, 0], [4, 1], [2, 3], [3, 1]]
      44 |   puts "Kahn's topological order: #{topo_sort_kahn(6, edges)}"
      45 |   puts "DFS-based topological order: #{topo_sort_dfs(6, edges)}"
      46 | end

       1 | # Cycle detection for both directed graphs (via a 3-color/state DFS)
       2 | # and undirected graphs (via parent-tracking DFS).
       3 | def has_cycle_directed?(num_vertices, edges)
>>>    4 |   adj = Hash.new { |h, k| h[k] = [] }
       5 |   edges.each { |u, v| adj[u] << v }
       6 |   state = Array.new(num_vertices, 0) # 0=unvisited, 1=in-progress, 2=done
       7 | 
       8 |   visit = lambda do |u|
       9 |     state[u] = 1
      10 |     adj[u].each do |v|
      11 |       return true if state[v] == 1
      12 |       return true if state[v] == 0 && visit.call(v)
      13 |     end
      14 |     state[u] = 2
      15 |     false
      16 |   end
      17 | 
      18 |   (0...num_vertices).any? { |v| state[v] == 0 && visit.call(v) }
      19 | end
      20 | 
      21 | def has_cycle_undirected?(num_vertices, edges)
      22 |   adj = Hash.new { |h, k| h[k] = [] }
      23 |   edges.each { |u, v| adj[u] << v; adj[v] << u }
      24 |   visited = Array.new(num_vertices, false)
      25 | 
      26 |   visit = lambda do |u, parent|
      27 |     visited[u] = true
      28 |     adj[u].each do |v|
      29 |       next if v == parent
      30 |       return true if visited[v] || visit.call(v, u)
      31 |     end
      32 |     false
      33 |   end
      34 | 
      35 |   (0...num_vertices).any? { |v| !visited[v] && visit.call(v, -1) }
      36 | end
      37 | 
      38 | if __FILE__ == $0
      39 |   puts "Directed cycle [0->1,1->2,2->0]: #{has_cycle_directed?(3, [[0, 1], [1, 2], [2, 0]])}"
      40 |   puts "Directed no cycle [0->1,1->2]: #{has_cycle_directed?(3, [[0, 1], [1, 2]])}"
      41 |   puts "Undirected cycle [0-1,1-2,2-0]: #{has_cycle_undirected?(3, [[0, 1], [1, 2], [2, 0]])}"

       1 | # Tarjan's algorithm for finding Strongly Connected Components in a
       2 | # directed graph using a single DFS pass with low-link values.
       3 | class TarjanSCC
       4 |   def initialize(num_vertices, edges)
>>>    5 |     @adj = Hash.new { |h, k| h[k] = [] }
       6 |     edges.each { |u, v| @adj[u] << v }
       7 |     @n = num_vertices
       8 |     @index_counter = 0
       9 |     @index = {}
      10 |     @lowlink = {}
      11 |     @on_stack = {}
      12 |     @stack = []
      13 |     @sccs = []
      14 |   end
      15 | 
      16 |   def run
      17 |     (0...@n).each { |v| strong_connect(v) unless @index[v] }
      18 |     @sccs
      19 |   end
      20 | 
      21 |   private
      22 | 
      23 |   def strong_connect(v)
      24 |     @index[v] = @index_counter
      25 |     @lowlink[v] = @index_counter
      26 |     @index_counter += 1
      27 |     @stack.push(v)
      28 |     @on_stack[v] = true
      29 | 
      30 |     @adj[v].each do |w|
      31 |       if !@index[w]
      32 |         strong_connect(w)
      33 |         @lowlink[v] = [@lowlink[v], @lowlink[w]].min
      34 |       elsif @on_stack[w]
      35 |         @lowlink[v] = [@lowlink[v], @index[w]].min
      36 |       end
      37 |     end
      38 | 
      39 |     if @lowlink[v] == @index[v]
      40 |       scc = []
      41 |       loop do
      42 |         w = @stack.pop
      43 |         @on_stack[w] = false
      44 |         scc << w
      45 |         break if w == v
      46 |       end
      47 |       @sccs << scc
      48 |     end
      49 |   end
      50 | end
      51 | 
      52 | if __FILE__ == $0
      53 |   edges = [[0, 1], [1, 2], [2, 0], [1, 3], [3, 4], [4, 5], [5, 3]]
      54 |   sccs = TarjanSCC.new(6, edges).run
      55 |   puts "Strongly connected components: #{sccs}"
      56 | end

       1 | # A* pathfinding on a grid using Manhattan distance as the heuristic --
       2 | # combines Dijkstra's guarantees with heuristic-guided search speed.
       3 | def astar(grid, start, goal)
       4 |   rows, cols = grid.size, grid[0].size
       5 |   heuristic = ->(a, b) { (a[0] - b[0]).abs + (a[1] - b[1]).abs }
       6 | 
       7 |   open_set = [start]
       8 |   came_from = {}
       9 |   g_score = Hash.new(Float::INFINITY)
      10 |   g_score[start] = 0
      11 |   f_score = Hash.new(Float::INFINITY)
      12 |   f_score[start] = heuristic.call(start, goal)
      13 | 
      14 |   until open_set.empty?
      15 |     current = open_set.min_by { |node| f_score[node] }
      16 |     return reconstruct_path(came_from, current) if current == goal
      17 | 
>>>   18 |     open_set.delete(current)
      19 |     neighbors(current, rows, cols).each do |neighbor|
      20 |       next if grid[neighbor[0]][neighbor[1]] == 1 # wall
      21 |       tentative_g = g_score[current] + 1
      22 |       if tentative_g < g_score[neighbor]
      23 |         came_from[neighbor] = current
      24 |         g_score[neighbor] = tentative_g
      25 |         f_score[neighbor] = tentative_g + heuristic.call(neighbor, goal)
      26 |         open_set << neighbor unless open_set.include?(neighbor)
      27 |       end
      28 |     end
      29 |   end
      30 |   nil # no path found
      31 | end
      32 | 
      33 | def neighbors(node, rows, cols)
      34 |   r, c = node
      35 |   [[r - 1, c], [r + 1, c], [r, c - 1], [r, c + 1]].select do |nr, nc|
      36 |     nr.between?(0, rows - 1) && nc.between?(0, cols - 1)
      37 |   end
      38 | end
      39 | 
      40 | def reconstruct_path(came_from, current)
      41 |   path = [current]
      42 |   path.unshift(current = came_from[current]) while came_from[current]
      43 |   path
      44 | end
      45 | 
      46 | if __FILE__ == $0
      47 |   grid = [
      48 |     [0, 0, 0, 0, 0],
      49 |     [0, 1, 1, 1, 0],
      50 |     [0, 0, 0, 1, 0],
      51 |     [1, 1, 0, 1, 0],
      52 |     [0, 0, 0, 0, 0],
      53 |   ]
      54 |   path = astar(grid, [0, 0], [4, 4])
      55 |   puts "Path from [0,0] to [4,4]: #{path}"
      56 | end

       1 | # Knuth-Morris-Pratt substring search: builds a "longest prefix that
       2 | # is also a suffix" (LPS) table to avoid re-scanning text on mismatch,
       3 | # achieving O(n + m) matching.
       4 | def kmp_build_lps(pattern)
       5 |   lps = Array.new(pattern.length, 0)
       6 |   length = 0
       7 |   i = 1
       8 |   while i < pattern.length
       9 |     if pattern[i] == pattern[length]
      10 |       length += 1
      11 |       lps[i] = length
      12 |       i += 1
      13 |     elsif length != 0
      14 |       length = lps[length - 1]
      15 |     else
      16 |       lps[i] = 0
      17 |       i += 1
      18 |     end
      19 |   end
      20 |   lps
      21 | end
      22 | 
      23 | def kmp_search(text, pattern)
      24 |   return [] if pattern.empty?
      25 |   lps = kmp_build_lps(pattern)
      26 |   matches = []
      27 |   i = j = 0
      28 |   while i < text.length
      29 |     if text[i] == pattern[j]
      30 |       i += 1
      31 |       j += 1
      32 |       if j == pattern.length
      33 |         matches << (i - j)
      34 |         j = lps[j - 1]
      35 |       end
      36 |     elsif j != 0
      37 |       j = lps[j - 1]
      38 |     else
      39 |       i += 1
      40 |     end
      41 |   end
      42 |   matches
      43 | end
      44 | 
      45 | if __FILE__ == $0
      46 |   text = "ababcabcabababd"
      47 |   pattern = "ababd"

       1 | # Rabin-Karp substring search using a rolling polynomial hash so each
       2 | # window's hash is computed in O(1) from the previous one.
       3 | def rabin_karp(text, pattern, base = 256, mod = 1_000_000_007)
       4 |   n, m = text.length, pattern.length
       5 |   return [] if m > n || m.zero?
       6 | 
       7 |   high_order = base.pow(m - 1, mod)
       8 |   pattern_hash = 0
       9 |   text_hash = 0
      10 | 
      11 |   m.times do |i|
      12 |     pattern_hash = (pattern_hash * base + pattern[i].ord) % mod
      13 |     text_hash = (text_hash * base + text[i].ord) % mod
      14 |   end
      15 | 
      16 |   matches = []
      17 |   (0..n - m).each do |i|
      18 |     matches << i if pattern_hash == text_hash && text[i, m] == pattern
      19 |     if i < n - m
      20 |       text_hash = ((text_hash - text[i].ord * high_order) * base + text[i + m].ord) % mod
>>>   21 |       text_hash += mod if text_hash.negative?
      22 |     end
      23 |   end
      24 |   matches
      25 | end
      26 | 
      27 | if __FILE__ == $0
      28 |   text = "GEEKS FOR GEEKS"
      29 |   pattern = "GEEK"
      30 |   puts "Matches at indices: #{rabin_karp(text, pattern)}"
      31 | end

       1 | # Longest Common Subsequence via bottom-up DP, with reconstruction
       2 | # of the actual subsequence by walking the DP table backward.
       3 | def lcs(a, b)
       4 |   m, n = a.length, b.length
       5 |   dp = Array.new(m + 1) { Array.new(n + 1, 0) }
       6 | 
       7 |   (1..m).each do |i|
       8 |     (1..n).each do |j|
       9 |       dp[i][j] = if a[i - 1] == b[j - 1]
      10 |                    dp[i - 1][j - 1] + 1
      11 |                  else
>>>   12 |                    [dp[i - 1][j], dp[i][j - 1]].max
      13 |                  end
      14 |     end
      15 |   end
      16 | 
      17 |   i, j = m, n
      18 |   result = []
      19 |   while i > 0 && j > 0
      20 |     if a[i - 1] == b[j - 1]
      21 |       result << a[i - 1]
      22 |       i -= 1
      23 |       j -= 1
      24 |     elsif dp[i - 1][j] >= dp[i][j - 1]
      25 |       i -= 1
      26 |     else
      27 |       j -= 1
      28 |     end
      29 |   end
      30 | 
      31 |   [dp[m][n], result.reverse.join]
      32 | end
      33 | 
      34 | if __FILE__ == $0
      35 |   length, sequence = lcs("ABCBDAB", "BDCABA")
      36 |   puts "LCS length: #{length}, sequence: #{sequence}"

       1 | # Longest palindromic substring via "expand around center", checking
       2 | # both odd-length and even-length centers in O(n^2) time, O(1) space.
       3 | def longest_palindromic_substring(s)
       4 |   return "" if s.empty?
       5 |   start, max_len = 0, 1
       6 | 
       7 |   expand = lambda do |left, right|
       8 |     while left >= 0 && right < s.length && s[left] == s[right]
       9 |       left -= 1
      10 |       right += 1
      11 |     end
      12 |     right - left - 1
      13 |   end
      14 | 
      15 |   (0...s.length).each do |i|
      16 |     len1 = expand.call(i, i)
      17 |     len2 = expand.call(i, i + 1)
      18 |     len = [len1, len2].max
      19 |     if len > max_len
      20 |       max_len = len
      21 |       start = i - (len - 1) / 2
      22 |     end
      23 |   end
      24 | 
      25 |   s[start, max_len]
      26 | end
      27 | 
>>>   28 | if __FILE__ == $0
      29 |   ["babad", "cbbd", "racecarxyz", "a"].each do |s|

      16 |   result = []
      17 |   rest_perms.each do |perm|
      18 |     (0..perm.length).each { |i| result << perm[0...i] + first + perm[i..-1] }
      19 |   end
      20 |   result.uniq
      21 | end
      22 | 
      23 | if __FILE__ == $0
      24 |   words = %w[eat tea tan ate nat bat]
      25 |   puts "Anagram groups: #{group_anagrams(words)}"
      26 |   puts "Permutations of 'abc': #{permutations('abc')}"
      27 | end

       1 | # Boyer-Moore substring search (bad character heuristic): compares the
       2 | # pattern right-to-left and skips ahead using mismatch information,
       3 | # often sub-linear in practice.
       4 | def boyer_moore_search(text, pattern)
       5 |   n, m = text.length, pattern.length
       6 |   return [] if m.zero? || m > n
       7 | 
       8 |   last_occurrence = Hash.new(-1)
       9 |   pattern.each_char.with_index { |ch, i| last_occurrence[ch] = i }
      10 | 
      11 |   matches = []
>>>   12 |   s = 0 # shift of pattern relative to text
      13 |   while s <= n - m
      14 |     j = m - 1
      15 |     j -= 1 while j >= 0 && pattern[j] == text[s + j]
      16 |     if j < 0
      17 |       matches << s
      18 |       s += (s + m < n ? m - last_occurrence[text[s + m]] : 1)
      19 |     else
      20 |       s += [1, j - last_occurrence[text[s + j]]].max
      21 |     end
      22 |   end
      23 |   matches
      24 | end
      25 | 
      26 | if __FILE__ == $0
      27 |   text = "ABAAABCDABABCABAB"
      28 |   pattern = "ABABCABAB"
      29 |   puts "Matches at indices: #{boyer_moore_search(text, pattern)}"
      30 | end
--- End Source ---

       1 | # 0/1 Knapsack: choose a subset of items (each usable at most once)
       2 | # to maximize value under a weight capacity, via bottom-up DP,
       3 | # with reconstruction of the chosen items.
       4 | def knapsack_01(weights, values, capacity)
       5 |   n = weights.length
       6 |   dp = Array.new(n + 1) { Array.new(capacity + 1, 0) }
       7 | 
       8 |   (1..n).each do |i|
       9 |     (0..capacity).each do |w|
>>>   10 |       dp[i][w] = dp[i - 1][w]
      11 |       if weights[i - 1] <= w
      12 |         dp[i][w] = [dp[i][w], dp[i - 1][w - weights[i - 1]] + values[i - 1]].max
      13 |       end
      14 |     end
      15 |   end
      16 | 
      17 |   w = capacity
      18 |   chosen = []
      19 |   n.downto(1) do |i|
      20 |     if dp[i][w] != dp[i - 1][w]
      21 |       chosen << i - 1
      22 |       w -= weights[i - 1]
      23 |     end
      24 |   end
      25 | 
      26 |   [dp[n][capacity], chosen.reverse]
      27 | end
      28 | 
      29 | if __FILE__ == $0
      30 |   weights = [1, 3, 4, 5]
      31 |   values  = [1, 4, 5, 7]
      32 |   capacity = 7
      33 |   max_value, items = knapsack_01(weights, values, capacity)
      34 |   puts "Max value: #{max_value}"
      35 |   puts "Items chosen (indices): #{items}"
      36 | end

       1 | # Longest Increasing Subsequence: an O(n log n) length-only solution
       2 | # using patience sorting (binary search over "tails"), plus an O(n^2)
       3 | # solution that reconstructs the actual sequence.
       4 | def lis_length(arr)
       5 |   return 0 if arr.empty?
       6 |   tails = []
       7 |   arr.each do |x|
>>>    8 |     pos = tails.bsearch_index { |t| t >= x }
       9 |     if pos
      10 |       tails[pos] = x
      11 |     else
      12 |       tails << x
      13 |     end
      14 |   end
      15 |   tails.length
      16 | end
      17 | 
      18 | def lis_sequence(arr)
      19 |   n = arr.length
      20 |   dp = Array.new(n, 1)
      21 |   parent = Array.new(n, -1)
      22 | 
      23 |   (0...n).each do |i|
      24 |     (0...i).each do |j|
      25 |       if arr[j] < arr[i] && dp[j] + 1 > dp[i]
      26 |         dp[i] = dp[j] + 1
      27 |         parent[i] = j
      28 |       end
      29 |     end
      30 |   end
      31 | 
      32 |   max_idx = dp.each_index.max_by { |i| dp[i] }
      33 |   sequence = []
      34 |   while max_idx != -1
      35 |     sequence << arr[max_idx]
      36 |     max_idx = parent[max_idx]
      37 |   end
      38 |   sequence.reverse
      39 | end
      40 | 
      41 | if __FILE__ == $0
      42 |   arr = [10, 9, 2, 5, 3, 7, 101, 18]
      43 |   puts "LIS length (O(n log n)): #{lis_length(arr)}"
      44 |   puts "LIS sequence (O(n^2)): #{lis_sequence(arr)}"
      45 | end
--- End Source ---

       2 | # (separate) number of distinct ways to make that amount -- two
       3 | # related but subtly different DP formulations.
       4 | def coin_change_min_coins(coins, amount)
       5 |   dp = Array.new(amount + 1, Float::INFINITY)
       6 |   dp[0] = 0
       7 |   (1..amount).each do |a|
       8 |     coins.each do |coin|
>>>    9 |       dp[a] = [dp[a], dp[a - coin] + 1].min if coin <= a
      10 |     end
      11 |   end
      12 |   dp[amount] == Float::INFINITY ? -1 : dp[amount]
      13 | end
      14 | 
      15 | def coin_change_ways(coins, amount)

      17 |   dp[0] = 1
      18 |   coins.each do |coin|
      19 |     (coin..amount).each do |a|
      20 |       dp[a] += dp[a - coin]
      21 |     end
      22 |   end
      23 |   dp[amount]
      24 | end

       1 | # Matrix Chain Multiplication: find the parenthesization that
       2 | # minimizes scalar multiplications when multiplying a chain of
       3 | # matrices, via interval DP.
       4 | def matrix_chain_order(dims)
       5 |   n = dims.length - 1 # number of matrices
       6 |   dp = Array.new(n) { Array.new(n, 0) }
       7 |   split = Array.new(n) { Array.new(n, 0) }
       8 | 
       9 |   (1...n).each do |len|
      10 |     (0...n - len).each do |i|
      11 |       j = i + len
>>>   12 |       dp[i][j] = Float::INFINITY
      13 |       (i...j).each do |k|
      14 |         cost = dp[i][k] + dp[k + 1][j] + dims[i] * dims[k + 1] * dims[j + 1]
      15 |         if cost < dp[i][j]
      16 |           dp[i][j] = cost
      17 |           split[i][j] = k
      18 |         end
      19 |       end
      20 |     end
      21 |   end
      22 | 
      23 |   parenthesization = lambda do |i, j|
      24 |     return "M#{i}" if i == j
      25 |     k = split[i][j]
      26 |     "(#{parenthesization.call(i, k)} x #{parenthesization.call(k + 1, j)})"
      27 |   end
      28 | 
      29 |   [dp[0][n - 1], parenthesization.call(0, n - 1)]
      30 | end
      31 | 
      32 | if __FILE__ == $0
      33 |   dims = [40, 20, 30, 10, 30] # 4 matrices
      34 |   cost, order = matrix_chain_order(dims)
      35 |   puts "Minimum multiplication cost: #{cost}"
      36 |   puts "Optimal parenthesization: #{order}"
      37 | end

       1 | # Subset Sum: does any subset of the array sum to a target? Includes
       2 | # a space-efficient existence check and a full reconstruction of one
       3 | # valid subset.
       4 | def subset_sum_exists?(arr, target)
       5 |   dp = Array.new(target + 1, false)
       6 |   dp[0] = true
       7 |   arr.each do |num|
       8 |     target.downto(num) do |t|
>>>    9 |       dp[t] ||= dp[t - num]
      10 |     end
      11 |   end
      12 |   dp[target]
      13 | end
      14 | 
      15 | def subset_sum_find(arr, target)
      16 |   n = arr.length
      17 |   dp = Array.new(n + 1) { Array.new(target + 1, false) }
      18 |   (0..n).each { |i| dp[i][0] = true }
      19 | 
      20 |   (1..n).each do |i|
      21 |     (0..target).each do |t|
      22 |       dp[i][t] = dp[i - 1][t]
      23 |       dp[i][t] ||= dp[i - 1][t - arr[i - 1]] if arr[i - 1] <= t
      24 |     end
      25 |   end
      26 |   return nil unless dp[n][target]
      27 | 
      28 |   subset = []
      29 |   t = target
      30 |   n.downto(1) do |i|
      31 |     if dp[i][t] && !dp[i - 1][t]
      32 |       subset << arr[i - 1]
      33 |       t -= arr[i - 1]
      34 |     end
      35 |   end
      36 |   subset
      37 | end
      38 | 
      39 | if __FILE__ == $0
      40 |   arr = [3, 34, 4, 12, 5, 2]

      42 |   puts "Subset found: #{subset_sum_find(arr, 9)}"

       2 | # find the cut lengths that maximize total revenue, via bottom-up DP.
       3 | def rod_cutting(prices, length)
       4 |   dp = Array.new(length + 1, 0)
       5 |   cuts = Array.new(length + 1, 0)
       6 | 
       7 |   (1..length).each do |len|
>>>    8 |     best = -Float::INFINITY
       9 |     (1..len).each do |cut|
      10 |       price = prices[cut - 1] || -Float::INFINITY
      11 |       if price + dp[len - cut] > best
      12 |         best = price + dp[len - cut]
      13 |         cuts[len] = cut
      14 |       end
      15 |     end
      16 |     dp[len] = best
      17 |   end
      18 | 
      19 |   pieces = []

      21 |   while remaining > 0
      22 |     pieces << cuts[remaining]
      23 |     remaining -= cuts[remaining]
      24 |   end

       1 | # Three ways to compute Fibonacci numbers -- naive recursion (O(2^n)),
       2 | # top-down memoization (O(n)), and bottom-up tabulation (O(n), O(1)
       3 | # space) -- benchmarked side by side to make the difference concrete.
       4 | require 'benchmark'
       5 | 
       6 | def fib_naive(n)
       7 |   return n if n <= 1
       8 |   fib_naive(n - 1) + fib_naive(n - 2)
       9 | end
      10 | 
      11 | def fib_memo(n, memo = {})
      12 |   return n if n <= 1
>>>   13 |   memo[n] ||= fib_memo(n - 1, memo) + fib_memo(n - 2, memo)
      14 | end
      15 | 
      16 | def fib_tabulation(n)
      17 |   return n if n <= 1
      18 |   a, b = 0, 1
      19 |   (2..n).each { a, b = b, a + b }
      20 |   b
      21 | end
      22 | 
      23 | if __FILE__ == $0
      24 |   n = 30
      25 |   puts "fib_naive(#{n}) = #{fib_naive(n)}"
      26 |   puts "fib_memo(#{n}) = #{fib_memo(n)}"
      27 |   puts "fib_tabulation(#{n}) = #{fib_tabulation(n)}"
      28 | 
      29 |   Benchmark.bm(15) do |bm|
      30 |     bm.report("naive:")      { fib_naive(n) }

      32 |     bm.report("tabulation:") { fib_tabulation(n) }

       1 | # Longest Common Substring (contiguous, unlike subsequence) via DP,
       2 | # tracking the best run length ending at each pair of positions.
       3 | def longest_common_substring(a, b)
       4 |   m, n = a.length, b.length
       5 |   dp = Array.new(m + 1) { Array.new(n + 1, 0) }
       6 |   max_len = 0
       7 |   end_idx = 0
       8 | 
       9 |   (1..m).each do |i|
      10 |     (1..n).each do |j|
      11 |       if a[i - 1] == b[j - 1]
      12 |         dp[i][j] = dp[i - 1][j - 1] + 1
      13 |         if dp[i][j] > max_len
      14 |           max_len = dp[i][j]
      15 |           end_idx = i
      16 |         end
      17 |       end
      18 |     end
      19 |   end
      20 | 
      21 |   [max_len, a[end_idx - max_len, max_len]]
      22 | end
      23 | 
>>>   24 | if __FILE__ == $0
      25 |   a = "GeeksforGeeks"
      26 |   b = "GeeksQuiz"
      27 |   length, substring = longest_common_substring(a, b)

      29 | end

       1 | # Egg dropping puzzle: with a given number of eggs and floors, find
       2 | # the minimum number of trials needed in the worst case to find the
       3 | # critical floor, via interval DP.
       4 | def egg_drop(eggs, floors)
       5 |   dp = Array.new(eggs + 1) { Array.new(floors + 1, 0) }
       6 | 
       7 |   (1..floors).each { |j| dp[1][j] = j } # 1 egg: linear search
       8 | 
       9 |   (2..eggs).each do |i|
      10 |     (1..floors).each do |j|
      11 |       dp[i][j] = Float::INFINITY
      12 |       (1..j).each do |x|
      13 |         # worst case: egg breaks (check floors below x) vs survives (check floors above x)
      14 |         worst = 1 + [dp[i - 1][x - 1], dp[i][j - x]].max
      15 |         dp[i][j] = [dp[i][j], worst].min
      16 |       end
      17 |     end
      18 |   end
      19 | 
      20 |   dp[eggs][floors]
      21 | end
      22 | 
      23 | if __FILE__ == $0
      24 |   [[1, 10], [2, 10], [2, 100], [3, 14]].each do |eggs, floors|
      25 |     puts "eggs=#{eggs}, floors=#{floors} => min trials in worst case: #{egg_drop(eggs, floors)}"
      26 |   end
      27 | end

       1 | # Word Break: can a string be segmented into dictionary words? Plus a
       2 | # memoized variant that returns every valid segmentation as a sentence.
       3 | def word_break?(s, word_dict)
       4 |   n = s.length
       5 |   dp = Array.new(n + 1, false)
       6 |   dp[0] = true
       7 | 
       8 |   (1..n).each do |i|
       9 |     (0...i).each do |j|
>>>   10 |       if dp[j] && word_dict.include?(s[j...i])
      11 |         dp[i] = true
      12 |         break
      13 |       end
      14 |     end
      15 |   end
      16 |   dp[n]
      17 | end
      18 | 
      19 | def word_break_all(s, word_dict)
      20 |   n = s.length
      21 |   memo = {}
      22 | 
      23 |   solve = lambda do |start|
      24 |     return [""] if start == n
      25 |     return memo[start] if memo.key?(start)
      26 | 
      27 |     sentences = []
      28 |     (start + 1..n).each do |endi|
      29 |       word = s[start...endi]
      30 |       next unless word_dict.include?(word)
      31 |       solve.call(endi).each do |rest|
      32 |         sentences << (rest.empty? ? word : "#{word} #{rest}")
      33 |       end
      34 |     end
      35 |     memo[start] = sentences
      36 |   end
      37 | 
      38 |   solve.call(0)
      39 | end

       2 | # from a shared Queue until a shutdown sentinel tells them to stop.
       3 | require 'thread'

       5 | class ThreadPool

      17 |     end
      18 |   end

      20 |   def schedule(&block)
      21 |     @jobs << block
      22 |   end
      23 | 
      24 |   def shutdown

      27 |   end

       1 | # Demonstrates a classic race condition (read-modify-write on shared
       2 | # state without synchronization) and its fix using a Mutex.
       3 | require 'thread'
       4 | 
       5 | class UnsafeAccount
       6 |   attr_reader :balance
       7 |   def initialize(balance)
       8 |     @balance = balance
       9 |   end
      10 | 
      11 |   def withdraw(amount)
      12 |     return false if amount > @balance
      13 |     current = @balance
      14 |     sleep(0.0001) # simulate work, widening the race window
      15 |     @balance = current - amount
      16 |     true
      17 |   end
      18 | end
      19 | 
      20 | class SafeAccount
      21 |   attr_reader :balance
      22 |   def initialize(balance)
      23 |     @balance = balance
      24 |     @mutex = Mutex.new
      25 |   end
      26 | 
      27 |   def withdraw(amount)
      28 |     @mutex.synchronize do
      29 |       return false if amount > @balance
      30 |       current = @balance
      31 |       sleep(0.0001)
      32 |       @balance = current - amount
      33 |       true
      34 |     end
      35 |   end
      36 | end
      37 | 
      38 | def run_concurrent_withdrawals(account)
>>>   39 |   threads = Array.new(10) { Thread.new { account.withdraw(10) } }
      40 |   threads.each(&:join)
      41 |   account.balance
      42 | end
      43 | 
      44 | if __FILE__ == $0
      45 |   unsafe = UnsafeAccount.new(100)
      46 |   final_unsafe = run_concurrent_withdrawals(unsafe)
      47 |   puts "Unsafe account: expected 0, got #{final_unsafe} (race condition may cause an incorrect result)"
      48 | 
      49 |   safe = SafeAccount.new(100)
      50 |   final_safe = run_concurrent_withdrawals(safe)

       2 | # control, and a simple round-robin scheduler resumes them in turn --
       3 | # unlike Threads, there's no preemption, so no locks are needed here.
       4 | class FiberScheduler

       1 | # Ractor-based parallel computation (Ruby 3.0+). Unlike Threads,
       2 | # Ractors run without a shared GIL between them, giving true
       3 | # parallelism at the cost of restricted object sharing.
       4 | def parallel_sum(array, num_ractors = 4)
       5 |   chunk_size = (array.size.to_f / num_ractors).ceil

       1 | # A bounded (fixed-capacity) buffer implemented with a Mutex and two
       2 | # ConditionVariables -- one to block producers when full, one to
       3 | # block consumers when empty. The textbook building block behind
       4 | # most thread-safe queue implementations.
       5 | require 'thread'
       6 | 
       7 | class BoundedBuffer
       8 |   def initialize(capacity)
       9 |     @capacity = capacity
      10 |     @buffer = []
      11 |     @mutex = Mutex.new
      12 |     @not_full = ConditionVariable.new
      13 |     @not_empty = ConditionVariable.new
      14 |   end
      15 | 
      16 |   def put(item)
      17 |     @mutex.synchronize do
      18 |       @not_full.wait(@mutex) while @buffer.size >= @capacity
      19 |       @buffer << item
      20 |       @not_empty.signal
      21 |     end
      22 |   end
      23 | 
      24 |   def take
      25 |     @mutex.synchronize do
      26 |       @not_empty.wait(@mutex) while @buffer.empty?
      27 |       item = @buffer.shift
      28 |       @not_full.signal
      29 |       item
      30 |     end
      31 |   end
      32 | end
      33 | 
      34 | if __FILE__ == $0
      35 |   buffer = BoundedBuffer.new(3)
      36 |   produced = []
      37 |   consumed = []
      38 | 
      39 |   producer = Thread.new do
      40 |     10.times do |i|
      41 |       buffer.put(i)
      42 |       produced << i
      43 |     end
      44 |   end
      45 | 
      46 |   consumer = Thread.new do
      47 |     10.times { consumed << buffer.take }
      48 |   end
      49 | 
>>>   50 |   [producer, consumer].each(&:join)
      51 |   puts "Produced: #{produced}"
      52 |   puts "Consumed: #{consumed}"
      53 |   puts "Bounded buffer correctly throttled production to capacity 3."
      54 | end

       2 | # threads can safely SET/GET/DEL/KEYS concurrently -- the core of a
       3 | # toy Redis-like server.
       4 | require 'thread'

       6 | class KVStore

      11 | 
      12 |   def get(key)
      13 |     @lock.synchronize { @data[key] }

      15 | 
      16 |   def set(key, value)
      17 |     @lock.synchronize { @data[key] = value }
      18 |   end
      19 | 
      20 |   def delete(key)
      21 |     @lock.synchronize { @data.delete(key) }
      22 |   end
      23 | 
      24 |   def keys
      25 |     @lock.synchronize { @data.keys }
      26 |   end
      27 | end
      28 |

      31 |   case parts[0]&.upcase
